### Native shell resolution, subject changes and mirror-bill evidence

The successor `data/processed/sd-wy-native-document-resolution-v1/` follows retained
publisher application code and configuration to the actual public APIs and document
links. Its **23 successful follow-up responses** preserve the client/API provenance,
**nine PDFs and all 96 pages**, including separate pinned OCR and rasters for all six
image-only veto pages. This resolves the two bounded application-shell observations;
it is not a statewide completeness finding.

For **SD 2018 HB 1132**, the native regular-session record and complete four-version
list recover the introduced PDF and three later engrossed PDFs. The introduced subject
is the alcohol-consumption age for military service members. All three later versions
instead concern alcoholic-beverage sponsorship/advertising and explicitly warn of
extensive amendment ("hoghoused"). The baseline and current native summary title match
the later subject, not introduction. A title-only classifier can therefore assign the
wrong filing-time policy even when the source title is faithfully imported. Both subjects,
every native version and the original title remain retained; no title is overwritten.
Two native primary-sponsor Republican observations are session-scoped, not certified
party-at-filing assignments.

For **WY 2020S1 SF 1004**, the native Senate API explicitly records **"S See Mirror Bill
HB1004"** and itself lists the obsolete House veto link present in the baseline. The
chamber mismatch is thus publisher-listed related material, **not a demonstrated import
error**. The Senate introduced text and the separate House introduced, enrolled, veto
letter and final-veto-summary PDFs are retained under their correct contexts. The House
bill has its own baseline identity; its documents are not silently reassigned as Senate
versions or treated as proof of the Senate bill's enacted status. Scanned-veto pages 1
and 6 were visually inspected, not all six; noisy OCR does not certify amounts, deletions
or operative provisions. The final summary also warns that its pagination differs from
the original enrolled act.

The imported-note parser now recognizes plural **"Amendments"** and **"Final Version"**
as later-version hints, without promoting any source to native-version or filing-time
certification. Inventory **v3** preserves every original link, version field, URL,
eligibility decision and row order from v2. Only **15,146** previously unclassified
link hints change, spanning **11,870** source-context measures in **nine** states;
all corresponding per-bill and per-state counts reconcile. A separate programmatic
successor also checks all five bill identity/context/title fields and original row order
against the unchanged 1,090,369-row source, preserving the preceding full link/version
reconciliation. These are metadata corrections, not new document recoveries or independent
semantic validation.

### Version-isolated bill inputs and complete SD input replay

The bill classifier previously concatenated current title, available summary and
introduced text without establishing their version alignment. This is unsafe even
when all three fields come from the same native bill. **2018 SD HB1132 / BillId 8918 /
DocumentId 47568** supplies a retained concrete counterexample: its introduced text
concerns military drinking-age eligibility, but its current native title concerns
alcohol sponsorship/advertising agreements.

`build_bill_input()` now uses **nonblank introduced text alone**. Title plus available
summary remains the fallback only when introduced text is absent or whitespace-only.
Insufficient introduced text abstains rather than being rescued by unrelated metadata.
The source columns remain unchanged; exact input text/hash, selected fields and
`introduced_text_only_else_title_summary_v2` construction identify the model input.
This does not certify timing, native identity, extraction quality or a label.
Reviewed Washington publisher-test exclusions still apply before inference.

A second repaired traceability defect discarded the model's ordered `output_codes`
and `rejection_score` while projecting bill predictions into the fixed export schema.
Both now survive classification and state-partition Parquet export alongside complete
scores/logits and abstention reasons. Unscored/baseline records keep these unavailable,
not fabricated. Focused regressions cover version-isolated text, exact whitespace/hashes,
insufficient introduced evidence, fallback compatibility and end-to-end learned-rejection
exports; the combined bill/classifier/document checks pass **164 tests**.

The new unlabelled candidate inventory accounts for **all 5,369 native SD introduced
documents / 15,308 distinct physical pages**. It retains **10,738 alternative inputs**,
one per document and reader, with complete page text, exact source/input spans, per-page
hashes, source metadata and shared native-bill groups. Alternatives are not extra bills
or independent split candidates. No current title/summary is prepended to a PDF input.

### Native Montana universe and draft/formal identity reconciliation

The largest apparent no-document-URL population needed an identity audit before more
text collection: all **7,641** Montana rows without usable retained URLs have LC
identifiers. The official 2024 drafting manual, physical page 65, distinguishes the
LC request number used during drafting from the House/Senate number assigned at
introduction. An LC source record does **not** establish another introduced filing,
but neither does an LC identifier establish that its underlying request was never
introduced.

The current statistics and drafting-procedure URLs returned HTTP 200 **maintenance
pages**, not statistics or procedural evidence. Their complete bodies and receipts
remain retained with that disposition. The actual Bill Explorer HTML links a public
client bundle that declares separate archive/current search APIs and session registries.
Following those exact contracts recovered all **15,830 native records across 18 complete
pages**, explicitly selecting the 2019, 2021, 2023 and 2025 regular sessions. The registry's
future 2027 cohort remains in the original response but is not admitted to this window.
Every POST body, URL, retrieval receipt, native object and page position is preserved.

| 2023 | 4,643 | 1,698 | 6,335 |
| 2025 | 4,495 | 1,759 | 6,159 |
| Total | 15,830 | 6,079 | 18,403 |

The native `offset` parameter is a **page index**, not a row offset. Retained diagnostic
requests using `limit=100&offset=100` report page 100 and physical offset 10,000 with
empty content for both API families. Complete collection instead enumerates page
indices, verifies declared totals, sizes, first/last flags and unique LC identities,
and uses bounded 1,000-row pages to reduce requests. Copying the client bundle's
row-offset-style loops would silently skip source records.

full native JSON and LC/formal aliases. **18,399** baseline rows have unique native

Of the 7,641 no-URL LC rows, **7,488** match native records with no formal identity,

### Montana printed identities contradict simple alias merging

Following the original document references changed the interpretation of two nonmatches.
Official PDF `266373` prints **2025 SB 6.1 / SENATE BILL NO. 6**, with B. Usher named
as introducer; PDFs `269545` and `269546` print **2025 SB 85.1 / SENATE BILL NO. 85**,
with M. Yakawich named. Their source overviews reference LC0239 and LC0442, respectively.
The retained current native records associate those requests with **HB 117** and
**HB 161**. Separately recovered PDFs `269566` and `270470` actually print those House
identities, with A. Regier and V. Moore named, respectively. The unnumbered LC0239 and
LC0442 drafts are retained too.

This is **not proof that either Senate identifier is an erroneous source alias**.
Nor does a numbered print or an `INTRODUCED BY` line prove an introduction event,
its date, or continuity with the current House record. Similar policy text, shared LC
references, PDF metadata dates and current enactment status cannot adjudicate that
history. All four original exact-key nonmatches remain unchanged; no Senate/House
record was merged, deleted or admitted to analytical filing counts.

The separate immutable conflict packet retains **seven distinct PDF byte hashes /
21 physical pages**, with all pages independently extracted by pypdf and PyMuPDF:
**42 reader-page records and exact printed-header spans**. It binds all original fields
for seven baseline contexts, ten exact recovered-document reference rows, all their
other version references and source overviews, and both complete current native objects
at their original page positions. Both SB85 document IDs produce identical complete
page text in both readers, but their bytes and PDF modification metadata differ;
text equality is not byte identity or permission to discard a source version.

The SR55 PDF and LC3368 legacy overview returned explicit **404** failures. The SR55
overview returned **200**, but only the Bill Explorer application shell with an empty
root, not bill-specific evidence. These responses do not resolve either identity or
certify LC3368 as a publisher test record. No visual-layout, operative-version,
filing-time, party-at-filing or policy-label certification is asserted.

A new direct archive-host check follows an indexed PDF search result without
mistaking that result for a currently retrievable original. Exactly **one** new
verified-TLS request, without retry or redirect, again returns **HTTP 404**.
Unlike the earlier helper's error receipt, the complete **49,280-byte response
body** and ordered parsed headers are retained and independently checked against

### Montana LC source rows excluded as additional formal filings

The filing consumers now share `exclude_mt_lc_source_requests_v1`, a narrow source-identity
guard rather than an introduction-status adjudication. An exact Montana LC request row
is not an additional independent formal filing, even when imported classification says
`bill`. This does **not** establish that its underlying request was never introduced,
delete any source row or evidence, label LC3368 a publisher test, or merge the contradictory
SB6/HB117 and SB85/HB161 formal records. Montana input frames missing a nonempty source
identifier fail explicitly.

The guard is wired through revealed emphasis, recorded outcomes, election shares and
tag validation, title-based terms, exact/near reuse **before candidate blocking**, and
direct source-tag validation/replication/trends. LC source-request rows do not reach the classifier.
Record exports preserve original fields and exact assembled input/hash, with
`source_draft_request`, an explicit `excluded` status and reason, and no model scores.
Coverage reports `n_source_draft_excluded`, retains the original attributed source-row
count and excludes requests from substantive/classified/abstention denominators.
State-focus and capability evidence carry the exclusion count.

The separate source census binds **all 1,090,369 source rows** and preserves all
**18,403 complete Montana contexts**. It identifies **12,321 LC request source rows**:
**7,343** pass the old classification-only outcome predicate; **12,321** pass the
election measure-type predicate. These are latent scope exposures, **not thousands
of demonstrated published double-counts**. Only 60 source rows nationally currently
carry D/R attribution, and **none is in Montana**. The guard therefore removes
**zero currently party-attributed rows**. Its effects on unattributed title-reuse
candidates were not quantified here.

All **12,321** request rows were exported as complete, unscored exclusions. Independent
replay re-reads the unchanged canonical source, reconstructs every national scope flag
without the producer's matcher, independently reconstructs both measure-type predicates,
and checks every source field, input text/hash, exclusion status and empty score/chunk
record. It also reproduces the session/classification accounting. No model was loaded
or called, and no existing analytical table, label, weight or threshold was regenerated.

## Training reservation enforcement

The training CLI now accepts an explicit `--source-reservation` pointing to a
manifest-bound reservation JSON. Before importing model libraries or downloading weights,
it verifies every packet file and excludes reserved platform-training organizations,
including alternate editions, as well as matching body or complete-document text hashes.
State/party metadata, explicit organization identifiers and organization-based groups must
agree. Reviewed state-platform training rows require an organization identity; external
national-platform groups do not silently become state organizations. Bills and reserved
evaluation rows are not excluded by platform-training reservations.

When supplied, the exact reservation path, JSON hash, packet-manifest hash and organization
list enter the saved training manifest. Omission is recorded as null; older runs are not
retroactively described as having used this guard. The six reserved organizations remain
ID-R, MT-R, WA-R, MN-D, WA-D and VT-D. At that checkpoint no labels or weights changed.
The subsequent reviewed use of complete retained KS-R, NE-R and HI-R native documents
is recorded below; their original source-context packet remains unlabelled evidence.

## Complete sponsorship-attribution missingness audit

The retained dump contains **21,993 party memberships**, of which **21,810 are entirely
undated**. Only **163 people** have any dated membership; 54 also have undated records,
and none of those mixed dated/undated people has only one recorded party. The bounds
contain no partial-length dates that a relaxed parser could safely rescue. None of
5,936 organization sponsorships names an explicit party organization in the retained
membership reference. These checked alternatives do not repair historical coverage.

The shared temporal resolver now exposes explicit evidence/missingness reasons while
preserving the existing `party_on_date()` interface and every party decision. Future
bill extraction also writes `party_resolution_reason`; current-party labels and
legislative-tenure dates are not substituted for missing party dates.

A complete replay preserves every original cell of **5,028,893 sponsorship rows** and adds
the reason, with **zero party or source-cell mismatches**:

| Resolution reason | All sponsorship rows | Recorded-primary rows |
|---|---:|---:|
| Undated membership | 4,257,879 | 2,052,642 |
| Missing person ID | 640,763 | 404,536 |
| Missing event date | 119,935 | 22,113 |
| Nonpartisan legislature | 10,014 | 8,317 |
| Active dated membership | 302 | 278 |

These are rows, not unique bills or people. Multiple primary sponsors can occur on one
bill. Reasons reflect the resolver's priority order: an undated membership can prevent
a later conflict check, so absence of a conflict reason is not proof of consistent
affiliation history. Missing person IDs also include source non-person sponsors; the
original entity type, name and organization fields remain present for further review.
The canonical bills still have only **39 D and 21 R** assignments under these rules;
unresolved co-primary sponsors cannot be rescued by resolved cosponsors.

The complete successor table, state/primary reason CSV, frozen code/tests and input hashes
are in `data/processed/bill-party-attribution-diagnostic-v1/`. Manifest:
`2f2406f260205225c3ff25ae14ea10ee9ba6ebabfd5bfb320b8538d331ff40ac`.
All **61** focused bill/outcome regressions pass. The current-legislator and methods
documentation now correctly distinguish current inventory from historical affiliation,
source-context years from filing dates, and no-primary fallback from unresolved-primary
backfill. No canonical source rows, bill party assignments or analytical results changed.
The source gap requires additional historical evidence, not weakening the join.

## Full-document text split isolation

Nine reproduced failures exposed a narrower remaining leak: distinct platform body
hashes could carry identical complete document text into different splits, while malformed
optional document hashes were accepted. The shared loader now validates provided
`document_text_sha256` values and checks this additional identity across both complete
training/evaluation manifests. The same guard applies before development calibration,
including original development documents later presented as independent tests.
Same-split platform passages and distinct bills sharing snapshot metadata remain valid.
All **68** ModernBERT regressions pass after the fix.

Full-document hashes remain optional for historical label compatibility; source-support
reports explicitly count missing values. This does not certify byte-distinct copies
without that metadata, differently extracted versions or near-duplicate families.
It does not infer a full-document identity from one selected passage.

`data/processed/training-evaluation-lineage-v2/` freezes both saved pilots rescored against
the complete metadata-only development successor and their exact original training evidence.
Both still make **27/50** decisions under unchanged policies. The ten selected platform
rows have source-bound full-document hashes; missing hashes in original training-manifest
development rows remain visible rather than being rewritten.
No weights, original labels, logits or thresholds changed. This is a provenance safeguard,
not an accuracy improvement or independent evaluation.
Manifest: `98952c935fc0553546ce4ddf8dc6dd0fc542b1f229dc3775f136e8266efc6e2c`.

|---|---:|---:|
| Bills, 40 cases | 22/40 | 23/40 |
| Platform passages, 10 cases | 5/10 | 4/10 |
| Pooled, 50 cases | 27/50 | 27/50 |

These counts include correct abstentions. Among platform cases, correctly accepted policy
decisions fall from four to two; correctly rejected cases rise from one to two.
Neither ten passages from one document nor provisional assistant judgments establish
population accuracy. The platform result is not an improvement simply because bill
decisions improve or the pooled score stays constant.

The joint pilot's **15** reviewed target-platform training rows comprise **six positive
spans across five of 21 topics and nine rejections**. All come from two **Democratic**
documents: California and Texas. The ten platform development rows all come from one
Texas **Republican** document. This is sparse, party/source-confounded support, not a
representative cross-state benchmark; it cannot isolate a party effect from document/topic
selection. Another run on the unchanged corpus cannot repair
the missing Republican target-platform training sources, sixteen unsupported topics,
limited geography or absent independent test/challenge references.

The original platform development rows omitted party and direct extraction-file metadata.
A separate complete **50-row** successor restores these fields for the ten platform rows:
party `R`, extraction path/hash and complete document-text hash. All **25** reviewed
training/development platform spans match their retained document extractions exactly,
and their three PDF body hashes reconcile with retained inventory captures. Topic,
rejection, rationale, input text/hash and original prediction bytes are unchanged; all
pooled and domain metrics remain identical. Bill party fields remain unknown rather than
being inferred from bill content. Original training labels/manifests remain immutable.

New training manifests and evaluation reports now expose per-split/domain external and
reviewed target support for every topic, reviewed rejection counts, platform-body
identities, recorded state/party and missing metadata. Explicit zero-row cells prevent
absent domains or held-out splits from disappearing from the audit. Counts describe
available supervision, not label correctness, independence or release eligibility.
All **59** ModernBERT regressions pass.

manifest `847989d20a0009158fa5689a2b065d671cee5fe81a4c83602e24c071b1a84993`.

## Checkpoint-bound evaluation lineage

Separate evaluation files can no longer bypass the training split guard. Both evaluation
and calibration now require the original checkpoint manifest and the complete label file
whose byte hash it records. The prediction's base-model run, training-label bytes and
taxonomy must match before the shared isolation check compares both complete manifests.
It checks record IDs, groups, normalized input and reviewed platform-body hashes, including
original development sources that must not later become independent test evidence.
Shared legislative snapshots still do not collapse distinct bills. Missing or mismatched
training provenance fails closed before a metrics/calibration report is created.

All **58** ModernBERT regressions pass, including ten new separate-file cases covering
each leakage identity, calibration, development-to-test reuse, altered labels/model/
taxonomy bindings and the legitimate shared-bill-snapshot exception. The unchanged real
pilot logits were rescored against their exact original training manifests in
`data/processed/training-evaluation-lineage-v1/`: both pass lineage checks and remain
**27/50**, using their unchanged research-default decisions. Original labels, weights,
logits, reports and thresholds were not rewritten. This verifies provenance isolation,
not label correctness, improved accuracy, independent gold or publication readiness.
Packet manifest: `598a88dcd7f050c134840d295a875eb295166243d478f4eb71fbae453c8cd4c7`.

## Initial platform-document split guard

The label loader previously accepted distinct passages from the same platform body in
different splits when their group IDs differed. A reproduced regression now fails closed:
reviewed target-platform rows require a source-body SHA-256, and a shared body cannot
cross train/dev/test/challenge splits, regardless of URL or group alias. Same-split
passages remain valid; multiple bills in one source snapshot are not collapsed into
one document. Preparation, training and evaluation all use this shared loader.

Both complete frozen pilot manifests still validate: **5,945** external-only rows and
**6,051** joint target/rejection rows. No old labels, predictions, scores or weights
changed, and no new training run was started. The ModernBERT test suite passes **48**
cases at that checkpoint. The initial check applied only to rows loaded together; it did
not establish disjointness
between a separately supplied evaluation file and a checkpoint's training corpus.
Byte-distinct versions, paraphrases, source-family review and independent gold remain
unresolved. The checkpoint-bound successor above closes the separate-file gap without
retroactively changing the original reports.

## Singular-resolution discovery and bounded follow-up

The follow-up also exposed a separate omission: discovery matched **resolutions** but
not **resolution** in a PDF filename or official link label. The shared discovery terms
and document-name scoring now recognize both. This corrects collection eligibility,
not substantive-policy classification, adoption or analytical admission.

Two frozen offline comparisons preserve the exact old/new discovery producers and every
candidate record. The first isolates the embedding repair. The second compares both
repairs against commit `127af74`: 208 identified HTML captures across 45 organizations,
within 339 selected source records, retain every previous candidate without any live HTTP
requests. It finds 119 additional reference observations at 83 distinct URLs across eight
organizations. Five observations are embedded references; the rest are ordinary links
newly exposed by singular-resolution matching. Thirty-five of the 83 URLs already have
retained responses in the selected inputs: these are not 83 newly discovered documents.
The replay uses captured requested URLs as bases, does not reconstruct historical redirects
or execute JavaScript, and is not a full-campaign or all-organization completeness test.

All **48 previously unretained URLs**, regardless of candidate score, were then requested
in a separate bounded run: 47 responses retained, one HTTP 404, across six organizations.
Forty-five responses have nonempty standard extraction; two retained DOCX files initially
have empty standard extraction. A separate standard-library ZIP/XML derivative recovers
both main-document texts without overwriting the originals. These are a submission face
sheet and a blank resolution template, not adopted policy merely because they contain
policy vocabulary or an unfilled adoption line. The alternate extraction preserves its
XML-member/source/text hashes and explicitly excludes layout, images and other XML parts.
The unresolved request is Nevada Republicans' `Nevada-Republican-Party-resolution-I-1142.pdf`;
its 404 is not evidence that the document never existed. Retained responses are unreviewed
candidates, not certified documents, dates or denominator members.

The three Florida PDFs exposed by the first comparison were also retained separately.
The official index reports July 19, 2026 adoption for the commemorative and economic-rights
resolutions and March 15, 2026 for the education resolution; upload-path months are not
adoption dates, and these remain source-reported events. Limited assistant role review
keeps the commemoration's concluding policy language visible rather than assigning a
blanket no-policy label. Its biographical issue mentions are not automatically party policy.
Fourteen economic-rights bullets and four education bullets are frozen with exact native
boundaries and controlling-resolution context. These **18 unlabeled candidate units**
must not collapse into one economics label, be called human gold, or be randomly split
across training and evaluation as if sibling bullets were independent documents.

## Four-state source recovery and institutional-index repair

A further bounded primary-source run covers **both parties in Iowa, Massachusetts,
Minnesota and Wisconsin**: 18 curated seeds and 55 one-hop document requests produce
73 capture records, 68 retained responses and five failures. All 1,512 observed references,
including excluded references and repeated parent sightings, are retained. This is neither
an exhaustive crawl nor eight certified operative platforms. Administrative documents,
proposals, older sources and Google viewer shells remain distinguishable from policy text.

Eighteen separate ordinary public alternatives retain another 15 responses, including
12 exact-file-ID Drive PDF downloads, one party-hosted Wisconsin PDF, one default-worksheet
CSV export and the Minnesota Republican apex homepage. The old Minnesota Republican
platform still returns 404 at the working apex host. Two older Wisconsin S3 objects still
return 403 over HTTPS. Original TLS/HTTP failures remain unchanged; no authentication,
permission/challenge bypass or disabled TLS validation was used.

Together these packets contain **91 request records, 83 retained responses, eight failed
requests and 54 actual PDF responses**. These are not distinct-platform counts. Offline
reconciliation checked every manifest/body/text hash, byte size, standard extraction,
seed/target set, parent binding, public-download derivation and strict CSV parse. It
reconstructed all reference observations and confirmed false training/analytical admission
and a null cutoff throughout. The CSV is the default worksheet only; other tabs and workbook
completeness are not verified. PDF parser warnings on an Iowa source remain visible;
reproducible extraction does not certify visual or textual completeness.

The two retained Massachusetts Republican scans have empty original standard extractions.
A separate OCR packet recovers both documents across three physical pages, retaining original
PDFs, page renders, per-page text/hashes and executable/traineddata/configuration provenance.
Every render and OCR result was independently reproduced, and all three renders were inspected.
Both documents display December 4, 2024 despite their 2025 upload paths; that is a displayed
document date, not independent adoption verification. OCR spacing/layout errors remain, and
the actual Tesseract 5.5.3 versus declared 5.5.2 runtime mismatch is still unresolved.

These sources exposed three further discovery defects. Existing secondary/deep mode now
recognizes generic party-document, constitution and bylaws indexes without making them strong
policy candidates. Recognized Drive file viewers are retained from ordinary anchors as well
as embeds, preserving exact case-sensitive IDs and parent evidence; unrelated external anchors
remain excluded. A filename/last-segment year now precedes upload-directory year hints: both
named 2024 Iowa platforms previously inherited 2025/2026 directory years. None of these hints
is an adoption date. Fragment-only skip links and popup actions are no longer document
candidates.

Thirty frozen before/after HTML replays against `464d64a`, using actual final URLs and zero
live requests, retain 34 newly visible candidate observations at 19 distinct URLs. Every added
candidate remains below the strong threshold. The only removed previous candidates are
12 fragment-only navigation observations. This is a selected-source comparison, not
full-campaign coverage; Wisconsin Democrats' existing `our-party` index already matched the
older secondary terms. The completed v1 comparison is retained; v2 additionally records the
fragment-navigation correction rather than silently overwriting the first comparison.

Limited Minnesota source-role review also exposes an unresolved discrepancy: the official
index reports a 100-item Action Agenda cap, but its linked 2026 PDF contains 108 distinct
heading-anchored policy candidates, matching all 108 passed numbered items in the exported
ballot. The worksheet contains 120 options overall: 110 reported passes and ten failures,
including non-numbered platform amendments and a petition option. Vote/percentage arithmetic
was checked, not independently verified as an election result. The retained official call
and proposed convention rules also state a 100-item cap; the current constitution permits
subsequent State Central Committee modifications but does not explain these eight additional
items. Do not silently drop eight items or certify all 108 from the reported results alone.

The 108 candidate spans preserve exact text offsets, headings, section context, source hashes
and ballot-item bindings. Two explicitly recorded title aliases and one inspected mixed-font
heading prevent silent mismatches. Pagination/layout material can remain in spans; they still
need cleaning and substantive topic review. They are **not gold labels, admitted training
examples or an independent test set**; sibling items share one document family.

After pushing the discovery repair, an actual follow-up of the three previously uncaptured
institutional indexes retained ten more responses: three indexes and seven PDFs, with
263 preserved reference observations and no failed requests. All bodies, standard texts,
hashes, parent references and the exact seven-target selection were independently checked.
A membership roster was explicitly excluded from this policy-index follow-up. Across these
three retrieval packets the bounded totals are now **101 capture records, 93 retained
responses, eight failed records and 61 PDF responses**, not 93 distinct policy documents.

Crucially, Iowa Republicans' newly followed About Us page links a **2026-labelled state
platform** that was absent from the earlier recovery; both it and the retained 2024 version
have seven physical pages but distinct bodies and text. Limited version review retains both
complete standard texts and a raw diff, not a count of substantive policy changes. The 2024
source ends with a separately headed four-item junior-delegate report. The 2026 source
instead includes a domestic-production statement with junior-delegate attribution inside
its Commerce priorities. Five exact role-evidence spans and the two relevant page renders
were checked. Do not flatten the older report into adopted party policy or blanket-reject
all junior-attributed language in the newer source. Neither the link nor a `FINAL` filename
establishes an exact adoption event or operative interval.

## Earlier recovery v4 checkpoint and exact native-field preservation

The new immutable `data/processed/recovery-source-inventory-v4/` combines the preserved
309-record baseline with eight explicit capture packets. It retains **488 capture records,

All 100 organization rows remain present; the other 45 have no retained response in this
selected union, not an evidenced absence of party policy. This is still not all campaign
evidence. Seven distinct bodies have OCR alternatives and two have DOCX main-document
text, separately typed and never counted as new source captures.

The shared validator checks every successor raw body, byte count, native/alternative text
hash, source binding, type/method compatibility, organization count and non-admission flag.
The producer refuses existing outputs. Exact comparison against all 309 baseline records
also preserves empty failed-native strings versus nulls: the initial inventory v3 is
superseded because it normalized that distinction. Its packet and browser snapshot remain
unchanged, not silently repaired. Version v4 adds a producer invariant and regression cases
covering both representations. Original bodies, standard text and four explicit inherited
legacy-NaN normalizations remain unchanged.

That checkpoint's local build is `data/preview-recovery-v3/`. Chromium compared all 100 exported
partitions, all nine distinct alternative extractions and all 19 failures; both complete
gzip downloads reproduce the frozen 488-record recovery stream and 2,091-record archive
byte-for-byte. Additional checks cover legacy OCR compatibility, literal markup in native,
OCR and DOCX text, pagination, stale-response errors, keyboard access, no-JavaScript
downloads and 390-pixel layout. The focused connected suite now passes 229 cases.

The missing canonical capability JSON/CSV reports were regenerated without inferring any
analytical results: all eleven questions remain unsupported, uncertified and cutoff-null.
That checkpoint's full reproducibility run failed **35 of 316 checks**, principally missing
canonical analytical artifacts plus an OCR executable absent from the default PATH.
The isolated OCR runtime and its declared/actual version mismatch remain separate evidence,
not a claim that the default runtime check passed. Neither source recovery nor interface
verification certifies classifier accuracy, operative coverage or comparative findings.

its result binds the build manifest and original complete capture stream.
All 100 recovery partitions and 309 complete records were independently reconciled with

The 179 focused site, coverage, collector, registry and discovery tests pass.
This is interface verification, not model accuracy,
operative-platform certification or full-campaign coverage.

`data/processed/source-version-visual-review-v1/` freezes five **limited assistant reviews**

The New Mexico Republican PDF's physical page 4 has blank signature/adoption fields;

`docs/WEBSITE.md` now records a concrete publication handoff against the locally inspected

## Claim disposition

The README preserves previous numbers and figures for comparison, but its warning applies to
**every old analytical result**, including stated-versus-filed, outlier, trend, election,
outcome, reuse and roll-call claims. They are unresolved until rebuilt and reviewed.
The old 62% top-1 / 78% top-2 figures do not validate the replacement or establish reliable
topic-specific performance. Source subject-tag agreement is not automatically accuracy,
independent validation, or an upper bound.

The existing 50-plank snapshot is a known, previously inspected diagnostic set. It is not
training data or a fresh blind test. New assistant/local-model judgments, if collected, must
retain that reviewer type and must not be described as human gold.

Dependency metadata was intentionally updated to declare Transformers explicitly. The
manifest hashes identify that change; they do not approve the analytical findings.
The lock is consistent offline. The project environment was restored from a hashed export
of the lock using an alternate package index after the default package host failed.
The original feasibility run used Transformers 4.57.6. A separate run now verifies
Transformers 5.14.1, Torch 2.13.0, NumPy 2.5.1 and PyArrow 25.0.0. It exposed and repaired
removed model-constructor/tokenizer APIs; real local checkpoint inference is also covered.
These checks demonstrate runtime feasibility, not political-topic training or accuracy.

PostgreSQL 18.6 and Tesseract 5.5.3 are isolated under `data/tools/`; no global installation
or database server is required. The OCR version differs from the legacy reproducibility
manifest and remains an explicit audit failure, not a silently updated requirement.
The full test run still fails its real-artifact audit because declared datasets/results
are missing. Other code checks must not be substituted for that missing research evidence.

## Legislative extraction contract

The extractor now stages all eleven bill, sponsorship, action, vote and richer-evidence
tables before promotion. Its source-hashed manifest starts incomplete, existing artifacts
cannot be overwritten, and successful extraction is explicitly **not source completeness
or scientific certification**. An interrupted run retains its staging directory and the
original dump; select a new output directory rather than overwriting its evidence.

PostgreSQL COPY parsing now fails on malformed rows, missing source-table blocks and
unterminated blocks instead of silently dropping evidence. Control escapes and encoded
UTF-8 are preserved. Abstracts, source URLs, versions and version links retain original IDs,
notes and dates with exact bill joins and per-bill availability counts. Empty URL fields
do not count as available references. Snapshot abstracts and signed-act references are
**not automatically filing-time-safe**, and version text has not yet been downloaded.

The extraction manifest intentionally leaves the analytical cutoff unset. Retaining source
context from sessions overlapping 2018 does not mean every retained row belongs in a
2018-onward analytical denominator. Actual dates, missingness and session completeness must
be measured after extraction.

## Measured source limitations after extraction

Of 1,090,369 retained source-context records, 7,594 precede 2018 and must not enter an
unqualified 2018-onward denominator. There are 24,661 missing first-action dates and
14 latest-action dates after the October 18 observation date. Future dates may represent
scheduled effectiveness rather than corrupt data; they require source/action review.

The snapshot supplies nonempty abstracts for 376,070 measures and version references for
1,069,117. The retained sidecars contain 388,389 abstracts, 1,743,057 source URLs,
1,899,909 version records and 2,617,690 version links. Reference availability is not full-text
retrieval or proof that a summary describes the introduced version.

**Historical party attribution is a major unresolved source gap, not a reason to restore the
old current-party fallback.** The dump has 21,993 party memberships, of which 21,810
have no date bounds. Under the strict dated-membership rule, 1,081,990 measures remain
unknown; only 39 D, 21 R and two other-party measures resolve, while Nebraska's 8,317
remain nonpartisan. These outputs cannot support D/R agenda comparisons.

A second source was recovered rather than assuming the dump was the only option:
the CC0 Open States people repository at revision
`bf4caf105dc0e4fbd52d06e970ec264540f5da97` (September 24, 2026).
Its archive SHA-256 is
`f0e96e3c5737d25852c7d7b242dce71f34f8a3b7c8dc271dc4fc464f6774b5cf`.
The inspected state legislature/retired files contain 21,886 people; 20,760 party records
are undated, and only 183 have any date bound. This source therefore does not by itself
repair the temporal gap. Legislative tenure and file creation/update dates are not silently
substituted for party-affiliation dates.

Reproduce the source audit, including the alternative-source check, with:

```bash
PG_RESTORE="$PWD/data/tools/Postgres.app/Contents/Versions/18/bin/pg_restore" \
uv run python -m state_politics.analysis.audit_bills \
  --observed-on 2026-10-18 \
  --people-archive data/raw/openstates/people-bf4caf105dc0e4fbd52d06e970ec264540f5da97.tar.gz \
  --people-revision bf4caf105dc0e4fbd52d06e970ec264540f5da97 \
  --people-sha256 f0e96e3c5737d25852c7d7b242dce71f34f8a3b7c8dc271dc4fc464f6774b5cf
```

The audit checks completed-extraction hashes before reading data, requires exact bill/evidence
joins, and exports all 50 state rows, 807 observed state/session/filing-year cells and
1,090,369 per-bill flag rows. Zero retained rows would describe source coverage, not prove an
official zero. The original measures, text, references and unresolved affiliations remain
available for further recovery and review.

## Recorded-action missingness correction

The original extraction labelled 44,425 measures `no_action_data`, but only 24,661 had
zero action records. The other **19,764 measures across 30 states** had recorded actions
without source action classifications. These are now explicitly
`action_stage_unclassified`, not missing, pending, failed or enacted. All other recorded
outcomes and bill fields remain unchanged.

The correction reconciled per-bill action counts against all **9,002,451 retained action
records**, after verifying both source artifact hashes against the completed extraction
manifest. The original eleven-table extraction bundle remains untouched. A separate
`data/processed/bill-outcome-correction-v1/` contains the full corrected `bills.parquet`,
the exact changed rows and a hash-bound `outcome_correction_manifest.json`.

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m state_politics.bills.outcomes \
  --source-dir data/processed \
  --out-dir data/processed/bill-outcome-correction-v1
```

Use that corrected bill path explicitly in subsequent outcome analysis. The correction
does not certify chronology, veto overrides, final disposition, source completeness or
historical party attribution; those independent audit questions remain open.

## Provisional supervised-label preparation

The three retained CAP files contain 508,287 rows. They are not interchangeable with the
adapted project taxonomy: reproductive-health, tax, election-access/administration and
family/culture boundaries require review. Original and harmonized code columns can differ;
inconsistent major/subtopic combinations also exist. The initialization preparer verifies
the exact source hashes, quarantines known incompatibilities, encoding damage and short
context-dependent units, excludes duplicate/close target text, and retains the original
codes and source-row references for selected records. It does not turn an unflagged row into
project gold.

The reviewed culture migration is CAP `23/2300` from legacy PAP `6/609`, not equality between
the two code columns. Current local preparation selects 5,895 external initialization units
across all 21 project topics, capped at 100 per source/topic and dated 2000 onward.
**Culture has only ten selected examples**, and these cover arts, not the adapted category's
full family/moral scope. This remains a serious training-support limitation.

The official CAP source-use statement was retained with provenance as
`raw/cap/source_use.html`. Project-generated variables carry CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 terms;
original-text rights can also apply. The platform codebook identifies its source units as
quasi-sentences, not necessarily standalone state-level policy commitments.
Source/model redistribution is not approved by this research preparation.

A 200-unit diagnostic packet contains two bills per state and 100 units from the newly
recovered official 2026 Texas Republican platform. The latter is one document, not a
representative state-platform sample. Fifty initial units (40 titles and ten platform units)
have explicit assistant-review identities, dates, rationales and exact evidence bindings,
including non-policy/mixed/insufficient-evidence abstentions. All are development diagnostics;
none are independent human gold or a blind certification test. The user was unavailable
to choose a reviewer arrangement; no human-review commitment is assumed.

The Texas PDF exposes 285 consecutively numbered policy planks. Paragraph-only extraction
had collapsed the document into 73 large units. Number-aware extraction now retains those
285 planks separately and preserves surrounding front matter and appendices: 299 total
source units, not 299 verified policy commitments. Source offsets remain exact, and
front matter is not silently dropped or labelled as substantive policy.

The dataset and review artifacts remain ignored under `data/labels/`. The preparer CLI is:

```bash
uv run python -m state_politics.analysis.prepare_topics \
  --reviewed-target data/labels/target-pilot-2026-10-18/reviewed_dev.jsonl \
  --out data/labels/cap-initialization-pilot-v2 \
  --per-source-topic 100
```

This prepares a research initialization corpus, not a certified replacement classifier.
Independent target review, a finalized rubric, broader state/party examples, family leakage
review, calibration and empirical release gates remain outstanding.

## First actual trained-model diagnostic

The official revision-pinned English ModernBERT-base encoder and classifier head completed
three epochs on `data/labels/cap-initialization-pilot-v2/labels.jsonl`: 5,895 external-coded
training units, batch four, 1,024-token budget, learning rate `2e-5`, seed `20261018`.
The saved `data/models/modernbert-cap-pilot-v1/` reloads through the strict research loader.
Its manifest verifies an actual encoder-parameter update, dataset/code hashes and three
epoch-history entries. This is a real topic-training run, distinct from the earlier
synthetic feasibility checkpoints.

**It has not demonstrated an overall improvement.** The following comparison uses the same
50 provisional assistant-reviewed development units, not independent human gold.
Thresholds were not tuned after inspecting the results: the new model uses its recorded
research defaults of temperature 1.0, confidence 0.8 and margin 0.2; the historical
nearest-description baseline uses cosine similarity 0.2.

| Diagnostic | Historical MiniLM | Trained ModernBERT pilot |
|---|---:|---:|
| Classified / all eligible | 39 / 50 (78%) | 40 / 50 (80%) |
| Correct topics / classified | 19 / 39 (48.72%) | 20 / 40 (50%) |
| Correct topics / all eligible | 19 / 50 (38%) | 20 / 50 (40%) |
| Correct abstentions / reference abstentions | 8 / 20 | 7 / 20 |
| Correct topic or correct abstention / all eligible | 27 / 50 (54%) | 27 / 50 (54%) |
| Bill decision accuracy | 23 / 40 (57.5%) | 22 / 40 (55%) |
| Platform-unit decision accuracy | 4 / 10 (40%) | 5 / 10 (50%) |

The ten platform units come from one Texas Republican document. Three inputs exceed the
legacy model's 256-token budget; its scores are cosine similarities, not calibrated
probabilities. These tiny development slices do not establish representative accuracy,
statistical superiority or publication readiness for either model.

The trained model's 23 reference disagreements comprise 13 accepted inputs whose reference
abstains, seven wrong topics and three missed topics through abstention. It confidently
assigns topics to committee lists, an honorific resolution and a retrospective report.
For example, `DNR-VARIOUS` receives a health prediction above 0.99 despite insufficient
evidence. Raising confidence alone is not an established remedy; substantive boundaries
and rejection need reviewed training evidence, not just a different encoder.

The retained abstract sidecar supplies evidence for 14 of the 40 development bills and six
of the 18 bill errors. A separate, explicitly post-hoc enrichment inspection fed the full
retained abstracts to the unchanged checkpoint. Agricultural overtime changed from
agriculture to labor; expungement changed from macroeconomics to criminal justice.
The honorific resolution still received a substantive topic, and cultural facilities
remained misclassified. Richer text can address some evidence shortages but did not solve
rejection or category support in these inspected cases.

Full error units, original review rationales, both input tiers, source abstract IDs,
predictions and hashes are retained under
`data/processed/topic-pilot-v1/posthoc-evidence-review-v1/`. This targeted inspection is
not a new test set or an improved accuracy estimate. Title-only references are not
automatically valid for enriched text; some previously ambiguous titles now have additional
substantive evidence. Abstract timing is unverified and cannot establish filing-time attention.
No labels, weights or thresholds were changed to obtain a better-looking score.

## Explicit rejection and separate target-domain training

The first pilot was trained only on policy topics: confidence thresholds alone cannot
teach it what a committee list or honorary resolution is. Training now accepts an explicit
null target only for reviewed target evidence with a `non_policy`, `mixed_policy` or
`insufficient_evidence` reason. Unreviewed external "other" codes and unresolved taxonomy
disputes are not converted into rejection examples.

When such examples are present, the head has 21 unchanged policy outputs plus one learned
rejection output. Manifest schema 2 declares the exact output map and rejection support;
schema-1 policy-only checkpoints remain loadable. A winning rejection yields topic null,
not a 22nd counted policy category. Predictions retain the full output map, logits and
rejection probability. Evaluation and development calibration preserve this map and include
reviewed null references in probability losses when that output exists. Existing uncertainty
and chunk-disagreement abstentions remain in force; no automatic certification is added.

All **128 complete inputs** in `data/labels/target-adaptation-pilot-v1/` received explicit
assistant semantic review: 106 were selected for provisional training and 22 quarantined
without labels. Selected training contains 57 policy examples and 49 rejection examples
(40 non-policy, eight insufficient-evidence, one mixed). Every selected record retains its
full input, source binding, reviewer identity/date and individual rationale. Keyword and
length strata were used only to sample additional titles, not to assign their labels:
the sampled "recognizances" bill is substantive criminal justice, for example.

The 50 original development judgments remain unchanged. Their IDs, groups and exact/close
text were reserved; no Texas Republican development document was split into training.
The two additional platform documents are California Democratic 2026 and Texas Democratic
2026-2028, distinct from the legacy diagnostic's California/Texas documents. Legacy diagnostic
texts were also reserved. These checks do not rule out paraphrased bill-family dependence.

`data/labels/cap-target-rejection-pilot-v1/` contains 6,001 training records: the same 5,895
external initialization units plus 106 reviewed target units, with the unchanged 50-unit
development set. Culture still has only ten arts examples; a learned rejection head does
not repair that deficiency or certify mixed-policy detection from one mixed example.
The reviews are assistant judgments, not independent human gold. Rubric disputes, broken
unit boundaries and ambiguous source context remain in the separate quarantine rather
than being silently taught as non-policy.

### Saved/reloaded rejection-aware result: no overall improvement

The second actual run completed at **19:02:55 UTC on October 18, 2026**. All three epochs
finished on the 6,001 training records, with batch four, 1,024-token budget, learning rate
`2e-5` and seed `20261018`. Training losses were 1.5689, 0.7333 and 0.3236; decreasing
training loss is not evidence of better target-domain decisions. A real encoder attention
parameter changed. Schema 2 declares the unchanged 21 policy codes plus final null and
49 reviewed training rejections. Actual saved weights and tokenizer were hash-verified
and reloaded; the default loader still refuses this provisional checkpoint.

Before inference, the comparison retained the first pilot's exact research policy:
temperature 1, minimum confidence 0.8 and minimum margin 0.2. No thresholds or reference
judgments were changed after observing the result. Each prediction is joined to the
same complete reference text and checked input hash, not compared through aggregate totals
alone.

| Same 50 development records | Historical MiniLM | External-only ModernBERT | Target/rejection ModernBERT |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Classified / eligible | 39 / 50 | 40 / 50 | 29 / 50 |
| Coverage | 78% | 80% | 58% |
| Correct policy decisions | 19 | 20 | 15 |
| Precision among classified | 48.72% | 50.00% | 51.72% |
| Correct reference abstentions | 8 / 20 | 7 / 20 | 12 / 20 |
| Correct topic or abstention / eligible | 27 / 50 (54%) | 27 / 50 (54%) | 27 / 50 (54%) |
| Bill decision accuracy | 57.5% | 55.0% | 57.5% |
| Platform decision accuracy | 40.0% | 50.0% | 40.0% |

Relative to the first ModernBERT run, seven records newly match their references and
seven no longer do; 20 match both and 16 match neither. The five additional correct
abstentions are offset by five fewer correct policy decisions. Of the new candidate's
21 abstentions, seven are explicit learned rejections and 14 are low-confidence decisions.
Its remaining 23 errors comprise eight accepted reference-abstention units, six incorrect
policy topics and nine missed policy topics. Every remaining bill error and all ten
platform units were inspected against their complete retained text after evaluation.

The learned head correctly rejects the inspected platform title and editorial-committee
lists, but still accepts both genuinely mixed platform units. It also still accepts a
retrospective court-report submission as criminal-justice policy with approximately
0.99999 maximum probability. Agricultural overtime remains misclassified as agriculture;
expungement remains macroeconomics. The new candidate regresses on the foreign-law
court plank and dual-citizenship office-eligibility plank. These are concrete failures,
not grounds to lower thresholds or claim successful replacement.

Only 106 of 6,001 joint training records are reviewed state-domain examples: 91 bills
and 15 platform units. The external component contains 2,000 bills and 3,895 platform
units. This limited target support, the single reviewed mixed example, unsupported
family/moral coverage, unresolved rubric boundaries and possible bill-family dependence
remain material limitations. The 40 development bills and ten units from one Texas
Republican document are not a representative all-state test or independent human gold.
No third run or production migration is justified merely by a lower training loss.

The new probability report covers **50 policy-or-null references**, whereas the first
model's probability losses covered only **30 policy references**. Their log loss and Brier
scores must not be compared as if they used the same output space and denominator.

Persistent evidence is under `data/processed/topic-rejection-pilot-v1/`:
`evaluation_plan.json`, `checkpoint_verification.json`, `development_predictions.jsonl`,
`development_report.json`, `matched_records.jsonl`, `matched_comparison.json`, training
and evaluation logs, and exact evaluation/comparison scripts. The local checkpoint is
under `data/models/modernbert-cap-target-rejection-pilot-v1/`. Key SHA-256 bindings are:

| Artifact | SHA-256 |
|---|---|
| Combined label JSONL | `693195cc478fcc65003ef30da005824b2c884dc59fd9636cb5b26ea51ccf44fe` |
| Frozen research policy | `6eb22b67622d5106cafc947ab704605607602710d64e269bec83d6355b572c8f` |
| Saved training manifest | `ba90c19710b07df593dee5c10a402b76c68fb558c1a6c9230be1383ffa70ae08` |
| Actual model weights | `922bd7bb95374701760bacfe398302e8c3a50bce817fe6a933ff333496d96738` |

These generated files persist locally but are ignored by Git; the pushed audit records
their outcome and hashes, not a remote backup of the model or datasets. The scientific

successful training command nor passing structural tests closes that gate.

### Full-abstract development diagnostic: limited gains, substantial abstention

All fourteen available development-bill abstracts were reviewed in full, not just the
previously selected errors. Their complete rows were checked against the hash-bound original
Parquet source. New assistant judgments were frozen at **19:09:47 UTC on October 18, 2026**,
before fresh inference: eleven policy references and three abstentions. Three formerly
insufficient titles now support provisional policy judgments. These new references preserve,
rather than overwrite, the original title-only judgments. Previous model results and the
six-error inspection were already known; this is neither blind nor independent human gold.

Both input conditions below use **the same new full-evidence references**, including when
the classifier receives only a title. They are not the original 50-record scores. Each
actual checkpoint was reloaded, fresh title decisions reproduced its earlier decisions,
and the same unchanged research thresholds were applied to both input conditions.

| Fourteen bills, full-evidence references | Classified | Correct policy | Correct abstentions | Correct decisions |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| External-only ModernBERT, title | 11 / 14 | 5 | 1 / 3 | 6 / 14 (42.86%) |
| External-only ModernBERT, title + abstract | 11 / 14 | 6 | 1 / 3 | 7 / 14 (50.00%) |
| Target/rejection ModernBERT, title | 7 / 14 | 4 | 3 / 3 | 7 / 14 (50.00%) |
| Target/rejection ModernBERT, title + abstract | 5 / 14 | 5 | 3 / 3 | 8 / 14 (57.14%) |

The enriched rejection model classifies only **35.71%** of these records. Its five correct
accepted cases do not establish general precision. Both models recover expungement and
police-certification topics with fuller evidence; the rejection model also recovers the
AI/XR education pilot. Agricultural overtime improves only in the external-only model.
Workers' compensation and wastewater regress with enrichment, while the rejection model
still abstains on cultural facilities, bottle-bill miniatures and environmental outreach.
The external-only model also falsely classifies the enriched honorary measures.

Inputs reached 1,643 tokens and two chunks, with zero discarded tokens in the enriched
predictions. Full token coverage does not cure poor classification. Every source row still
has filing-time eligibility **not verified**: one summary describes a House Substitute,
another has a CD1 marker, and even original-version descriptions lack independently
verified availability dates. Paid military-service leave for public employees also remains
an unresolved public-workforce/leave/military-benefits boundary, not a manufactured label.

The packet, references, full inputs, all four prediction files, matched records, report,
log and exact preparation/inference code persist under
`data/processed/topic-rejection-pilot-v1/enriched-development-v1/`.
Training, calibration, analytical admission and scientific certification are explicitly
unauthorized for these diagnostic judgments. The unchanged original comparison remains
**27 / 50 correct decisions for all three methods**.

## Additional contemporary source recovery

Three separately retained official-source PDFs now support broader extraction review:

| Organization and document | Extracted characters | Baseline parser units | Largest unit, characters |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| California Democratic, 2026 | 156,442 | 167 | 9,555 |
| Texas Democratic, 2026-2028 | 186,814 | 235 | 5,402 |
| Texas Republican, 2026 | 296,224 | 299 | 12,840 |

These are source/parser counts, not verified policy commitments or full organization coverage.
The California and Texas Democratic PDFs emitted PDF object-recovery warnings. Front matter,
contents, quotations, extraction artifacts and mixed units still require review. California's
landing-page adoption description and filename appear to disagree on February 22 versus
February 23; the discrepancy is unresolved, not silently normalized.

The Texas Democratic homepage's `PARTY PLATFORM` anchor links to an opaque Wix PDF filename.
Discovery previously missed it for two independent reasons: it ignored the anchor label,
and its Wix-media noise rule excluded the legitimate hashed PDF. Discovery now retains
same-origin PDFs with substantive document labels and binds their link evidence to the
source-page URL and byte hash. Non-PDF Wix noise remains excluded. Collection preserves
the label during rescoring instead of discarding the discovery improvement.

Replaying the exact retained official homepage now discovers that PDF as a strong candidate,
with the original source-page hash. The result is retained as
`data/processed/contemporary-source-pilot/texas_democratic_official_candidate_v2.jsonl`.
The ongoing full crawl started before this repair and does not acquire the new behavior
mid-run. These supplemental documents have not been silently merged into the main corpus,
and a failed search still does not establish absence of a platform.

### Source-unit coverage is not coherent segmentation

The three-source baseline freezes **701 complete parser units** with exact source offsets
and all uncovered spans. No non-whitespace extracted characters are uncovered in any of
the three documents. This demonstrates extracted-text retention, **not** complete PDF
extraction, coherent commitments, valid topic labels or verified adoption.

Direct review found a structural bug: `policy_units` selected only the single longest
consecutive numbered run beginning at one. California contains eleven syntactically
qualifying runs of at least twenty items, but only its 41-item run was split. The repair
preserves **all** qualifying runs without lowering the existing threshold or treating
short/nested lists as independent commitments. A regression case reproduces the old
failure with two long sections and verifies exact, non-overlapping source spans and
retention of intervening short conditions.

The separately frozen post-repair packet contains **938 units**, not 938 certified planks:

| Source | Baseline units | After structural repair | Uncovered non-whitespace characters |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| California Democratic | 167 | 404 | 0 |
| Texas Democratic | 235 | 235 | 0 |
| Texas Republican | 299 | 299 | 0 |

Both Texas documents retain exactly identical unit records, not merely unchanged counts.
Nine limited assistant findings bind thirteen selected before/after units to their full
text, stable IDs, exact spans and hashes. They distinguish front matter/indexes,
parser-created bundles, sentence fragmentation and unresolved version evidence:

- California's Small Business and Banking lists still bundle nine and five numbered
  commitments. Its children/families sentence remains split between “regardless of”
  and, after a page marker, “financial or social status”.
- Texas Democrats have 156 units with multiple literal bullet lines. A reviewed foreign
  policy unit combines thirty bullets in 5,402 characters. Physical-page tracing below
  corrects its initial folio-based location.
- California's 9,555-character staff/contents block and Texas Republicans'
  12,840-character alphabetical index are not substantive policy commitments.
- Texas Democrats' attestation has a blank June day and blank amendment/section fields.
  This leaves version/adoption unresolved; it does not prove that the whole PDF is a draft.

Post-repair inspection also exposed **trailing-boundary contamination**: workers item 35
absorbs the next Children and Families heading and introduction. The multiple-run repair
does not solve that problem. Cutting at the first blank line was rejected because a valid
final commitment can contain multiple paragraphs. Source-page/heading provenance or
explicitly reviewed spans are needed before claiming coherent units. These failures
remain visible rather than being converted into training examples for “genuinely mixed”
policy rejection.

All three packets withhold training and analytical admission. Only the identified selected
units have received these limited boundary reviews; the full 938-unit packet is not
semantically reviewed. No original training/development input, reference, prediction or
threshold was rewritten, and the original 27/50 model comparisons remain unchanged.
No further training or certification is justified merely by the larger unit count.

### Source context is not automatically a classification improvement

A separately frozen, **unlabelled** diagnostic compares primary text alone with the reviewed
heading/preface context on all 39 inputs. The same saved target/rejection checkpoint and
unchanged research thresholds produce all 78 outputs; no new training, calibration or
reference judgments are involved. Exact inputs, logits, scores, abstention reasons and
source identities persist. Independent reconstruction verifies every decision from logits
and all tokenizer offsets within the saved chunk spans. Each input fits one chunk
(at most 129 source tokens), so no source tokens are discarded.

Adding context changes **nine of 39 decisions**: four accepted-topic changes, two
policy-to-abstention changes and three abstention-to-policy changes. Thirty retain the same
accepted topic. Acceptance is 36/39 primary-only and 37/39 with context; this is **not
accuracy** and neither higher acceptance nor unchanged decisions demonstrate correctness.
These purposefully selected cases share only two documents and include a repeated
commitment; no independent-example or representative-performance claim is made.

Concrete output changes make the remaining problem visible. California children/families
item 7 explicitly concerns young men's mental health, yet its prediction changes from
Health to Law, crime and justice when the governing preface is added. A Texas campaign-finance
bullet changes from Government operations to International affairs; the anti-BDS/compelled
attestation bullet changes from Civil rights and liberties to International affairs.
Conversely, the Asian-partner sovereignty bullet moves from an accepted Public lands and
water prediction to abstention. These are post-hoc observations, **not newly certified
reference labels**, a new test score or evidence for a universal context-removal rule.

The exact primary/context distinction must survive the data contract. Do not automatically
prepend headings in production, let a section title substitute for a policy-topic judgment,
or change model-selection thresholds on these examples. Representative reviewed targets,
context-precedence rules and evaluation of the eventual input construction remain necessary.

### Broad category names conceal narrow supervised support

Reconstruction of all 6,001 training rows agrees exactly with the saved checkpoint's
per-topic counts. Of 106 assistant-reviewed target rows, 91 are bills and 15 are platform
units. Only **six platform units are policy-positive**, spanning five topics:
Civil rights and liberties, Health, Environment, Energy, and Law, crime and justice.
The other nine platform units are rejections. Total target row count is not evidence of
broad target-platform policy support.

Category 23, named Culture, family and social issues in the adapted taxonomy, has only
**ten positive training examples**. All ten were read: they concern arts, cultural
heritage or artistic exchange, and all come from four Democratic national-platform groups
(2000, 2008, 2012 and 2016). There are no reviewed target-domain category-23 positives.
These supplied labels therefore do not establish supervised coverage of the adapted
marriage/family/religion scope. This does not prove a model can never generalize; it means
the broad category name and existing training count do not validate that generalization.

A separate original packet retains thirteen complete Texas Republican rubric candidates with their
original unit IDs, exact source slices and physical-page locations. They contrast school
religion, medical conscience, parental authority, child healthcare, disability services,
marriage officiation/definition, historic preservation, religious holidays, divorce, foster
care and adoption. No topic labels or training permission have been assigned. Extracted
page markers and an absorbed Mental Health heading remain in that unchanged original packet.
In particular, a family-related word does not by itself make
healthcare, rights or social-service policy a category-23 example.

Subsequent direct inspection of nine physical PDF pages supports a **separate**, assistant
boundary-only packet for all thirteen inputs. Four inputs change: planks 127 and 204 exclude
printed footer/navigation text, plank 144 excludes the following Mental Health heading,
and plank 212 excludes an intervening footer while retaining all four foster-care subitems.
Explicit source/exclusion spans account for every non-whitespace character exactly once.
The excluded heading remains evidence, not silently appended context. No topics, independent
gold, training admission or analytical admission were assigned. Whole native substantive
units may still cross policy topics.

matches these wrappers to the original frozen training file. The gates mean **review
required**, not 155 proven errors or automatic reassignment to Culture. Many crime,
land-management and business-regulation examples may retain defensible original topics.
Paid leave, rural broadband, ceremonial observances, heritage material and context-dependent
fragments expose distinct scope or rejection questions.

These are conservative preparation gates, not a finalized crosswalk or a certified rubric.

Five selected bill concerns were also checked against the **original CSV**, not merely the

VA therapist qualifications. Some major-12 assignments may be defensible even when the
family subtopic is doubtful; the mammography description presents a more fundamental
text/major-code concern. Code-version agreement therefore does not establish semantic
correctness. No corrected labels or independent reference judgments were invented.

The source-concern packet retains full original rows, exact frozen training rows, source

The training field named `source_csv_line` is a logical record index including the header,

### Preserve repeated units' actual source locations

Following the repeated-commitment review, a regression exposed a separate provenance bug
in the legislative-caucus fallback. It discarded the offsets returned by `policy_units`
and searched the full document for each unit's text again. Identical paragraphs therefore
both pointed to the first occurrence, even though they remained separate analytical rows.
The fallback now keeps the segmenter's original start/end offsets. This changes neither
unit text nor topic assignments and does not silently remove repeated commitments.

The regression verifies two distinct exact source locations, not merely that both source
slices equal the repeated text. Curated Kentucky/Maryland extraction keeps its existing
behavior: a normalized or synthesized input without an exact contiguous source match has
null contiguous offsets and retains the full source text. No recovered-corpus error rate
or regenerated caucus result is claimed; the missing analytical artifacts remain blocked.

### Recoverable discovery runs

The original full crawl has now completed its initial 100 organizations and archive retry
pass. Independently reconciled final outputs contain 54 candidate links, including 42
heuristically strong candidates across 35 organizations. Live homepage retrieval succeeded
for 91 organizations; all 100 archive searches remained unsuccessful. Failed live searches
were AR-D, IL-D, NJ-R, NM-D, NM-R, NV-D, OK-R, PA-D and WA-D. Neither a failed request nor
zero homepage candidates establishes that a platform is absent. Candidate strength does
not establish document identity, adoption, operative status or successful extraction.

The saved `data/processed/platform_candidates.jsonl` SHA-256 is
`caf33fc8c0a7eadc945cb85687bd1c78c1ead2a3b819cec56bff6da108d38770`;
`data/processed/platform_discovery_status.csv` SHA-256 is
`532fc673a0b214b87d42f4168c5cf8649fa1badc0dbb7c02c1cf3d291379b354`.
The CSV contains exactly 100 unique state/party pairs, and its candidate counts reconcile
to the 54 JSONL rows. These are local ignored artifacts, not a remote data backup. The run
predates the recovery controls below: homepage fetches retained hashes but not response
bodies, and final candidate/status files were written only after retries. Its completion
does not retroactively supply missing bodies or resolve any operative-platform status.

New discovery CLI runs retain homepage bytes in `data/raw/platforms/discovery/`, using
distinct filenames so a later response cannot overwrite earlier evidence. Provenance binds
the stored path, response hash and original retrieval time. Each organization also receives
an atomic JSON checkpoint before the next organization starts. Archive retry snapshots are
separate and preserve both the original live candidates and the retry result, including
explicit failures. Checkpoints include registry/code hashes and the exact run arguments.

The default checkpoint directory sits beside `--out`; `--checkpoint-dir` and `--raw-dir`
allow a separately named run. Existing checkpoint directories are refused. This is durable
recovery evidence, not an automatic resume implementation or completeness certification.
An explicit `--no-wayback` supports bounded live-only supplements without repeated failed
archive requests. Skipped archive searches remain distinguishable from successful searches;
disabling both channels is rejected.

### Completed discovery inventory collection and honest retrieval failures

A separately frozen collection attempted **all 54 saved candidate URLs**, including weak
and negative-scoring links. It retained 52 successful response bodies, with complete
extractions even for rejected candidates. Two retrievals failed: AZ-D's resolutions page
and DE-D's `/our-platform`; another Delaware page was retrieved. The automated content
filter accepted 25 rows across 22 organizations and rejected 27 retrieved rows. These are
filter outcomes, not verified platform identities, complete commitments, adoption or
operative status. All 100 organizations remain unresolved on operative-platform coverage.
Original production/frozen source corpora were not replaced.

The packet binds the original inventory, registry, legacy gap annotations, executable source
copies, exact arguments, raw responses and provenance. All 100 organizations have atomic
JSON checkpoints; the 54 candidate dispositions and failure totals reconcile exactly.
Complete extracted text and a separate automated-acceptance flag are preserved in
`extracted_candidates.jsonl`, so short statements and landing pages are not lost merely
because they fail the full-platform filter. Following their substantive links remains work
to do. The packet's per-file hashes were independently checked after completion.

Two reporting defects were repaired before this run: failed retrieval rows previously
counted as `candidates_rejected`, and `--report-only` dropped failed rows entirely.
Reports now distinguish all-fetch-failed, mixed failed/rejected, unattempted strong
candidates and actual filter rejections. `n_fetch_failed` and `n_rejected` expose their
counts, including failures in organizations with some accepted evidence. The legacy
`n_fetched` field still counts candidate attempts, not successful downloads. Resume excludes
old failed rows before retrying; report-only retains them without issuing network requests.
The coverage figure uses distinct unresolved-status markers and no longer claims that
rejection proves absence, or that an old hardcoded date is the collection cutoff.

New collector CLI runs checkpoint each organization before starting the next. Checkpoints
bind candidate/registry/code hashes, exact arguments and all retained/retried outcomes;
operative status remains explicitly unresolved. The default directory is unique under
`--out-dir`, or `--checkpoint-dir` selects a fresh explicit directory. Existing checkpoint
directories are refused. These files support manual recovery, not automatic checkpoint
resume or scientific certification.
