### Short resolutions and corrected gap findings

A separate live-only Kentucky/Maryland/New Jersey/Pennsylvania supplement completed eight
organization searches: seven homepages were retrieved, Pennsylvania Democratic returned
HTTP 403, and only one weak New Jersey Democratic candidate was found. Those results do not
establish that the organizations publish no policy material.

Following official nested pages recovered eight additional sources with retained original
bytes, hashes and complete extracted text. Pennsylvania's Summer 2025 meeting page links
three resolution proposals. The official minutes document the meeting as **May 31, 2025**,
despite `June` in their filename. They report adoption of three resolutions and creation
of a Platform Committee, not the exact adopted wording or individual roll-call tallies.
The proposal list and adoption descriptions do not completely match.

Two linked proposals contain only 2,058 and 2,260 extracted characters and fail the
collector's 2,500-character full-platform cutoff. The longer boycott proposal passes the
automatic heuristic despite containing a cover letter, editorial comments and tracked
changes. This demonstrates both why a rejected short document is not absence evidence
and why heuristic confirmation is not adoption/version verification. All three remain
separate proposals, with the draft quarantined; no global threshold was lowered and none
was added to analytical counts or training.

Kentucky Democratic's official About page also supplies a substantive short position
statement, not a full adopted platform. The PA-D and KY-D findings in
`conf/platform_gaps.yml` now record these distinctions and exact source-byte hashes.
The gap CSV preserves that evidence as JSON rather than dropping it during regeneration.
Other July absence claims remain legacy findings, not newly confirmed October absences.
The authored-input checksum was intentionally updated for these reviewed changes; this
does not resolve the remaining scientific/artifact audit failures.

`processed/contemporary-gap-source-review-v1/` contains the complete captures and review
rationales, exact capture/review code, provenance and a separate
`source_coverage_reviewed_gaps.json`. The latter preserves all 100 organizations and leaves
the analytical cutoff and every operative-platform verification unresolved. Complete
review of the MDGOP bylaws remains pending; the later follow-up records only a limited
review of their platform-adoption section, not a policy platform.
Following the Pennsylvania index's resolutions/bylaws link returned HTTP 403; a separate
retry with declared browser headers returned HTTP 404. Both outcomes are retained and
the final-text/version gap remains unresolved. The raw minutes, not a search excerpt or
upload date, establish the meeting date and limited adoption evidence.

The ordinary collection CLI now also retains every successful candidate response under
`data/raw/platforms/documents/` (`--raw-dir` overrides it). Distinct paths preserve archived,
live, repeated and rejected copies, with URL/time/hash bindings in provenance; selected
rows retain `stored_path`. This repairs the previous hash-only collection gap without
silently admitting short statements, minutes or draft proposals to the analytical corpus.
Programmatic callers opt in with `raw_dir`; old parquet rows remain readable.

An actual three-proposal capture with the collector's default request headers was refused
with HTTP 403. A separately retained fresh capture using explicitly recorded browser
headers successfully exercised raw-body retention for all three PDFs. Its result reproduces
the two short rejections and the draft's heuristic confirmation; none is analytical
admission. Both runs, their exact code and complete provenance remain under the source-review
directory. Replaying retained bytes was not presented as a fresh network retrieval.

A fresh Texas Democratic homepage capture exercised actual byte retention and candidate
reload under `data/processed/contemporary-source-pilot/retention-pilot-v1/`.
The same official PDF remained strong after collection rescoring. The old running process
does not gain these changes retroactively, and no duplicate full crawl was launched.

A bounded two-organization Texas live-only supplement also completed through the actual

One candidate is strong: the Texas Democratic PDF. The Republican homepage's three weak
hits do not establish absence; its separately recovered 2026 official platform remains in

### Completed linked-source recovery and Unicode-safe evidence ingestion

The separate `data/processed/linked-platform-source-recovery-v1/` packet completed
**148 selected URLs across 30 organizations**, preserving 4,723 parent-bound link
occurrences, 146 response bodies and two retrieval failures. The collector accepted
82 responses heuristically across 23 organizations and rejected 64 retrieved responses;
all extracted text, including rejected content, remains retained. Exactly 100 organization
checkpoints and report rows reconcile. Independent verification matched all 260 manifest
file hashes, every selected URL/disposition and every retained body/text hash.

The two failures are a Georgia Republican convention-report URL and a Nevada Republican
resolution PDF, both HTTP 404. These establish failed URLs, not publication absence.
An upload-directory year is not an adoption date. Short substantive resolutions, national
platform copies, institutional rules, ceremonial material and loading/permission pages
remain distinguishable; automated acceptance does not establish document identity,
adoption or operative coverage. Oregon's annual resolution indexes still need further
link follow-through.

Real retained text exposed a separate ingestion defect: ten literal U+2028 characters
inside valid JSON strings were incorrectly treated as record boundaries by Python's
`splitlines()`. The fix uses physical LF boundaries in five JSONL consumers: training
labels, inference inputs, evaluation predictions, historical source-ledger ingestion
and local-preview ingestion. Focused regressions exercise literal U+2028, U+2029 and
U+0085 through inference/calibration/frozen evaluation and complete preview downloads.
Malformed JSON still raises; no broad exception handling or source rewriting was added.
The immutable extraction packet, input hashes, labels and model behavior remain unchanged.
The retrieval module's documentation now likewise distinguishes a 404 from evidence of
publication absence.

### Reconciled bounded recovery inventory and completed Oregon follow-through

Two immutable inventory snapshots now reconcile selected recovery packets without
conflating responses with platforms. Snapshot v1 contains 229 capture attempts,
219 retained responses and ten failed attempts. Snapshot v2 preserves every v1
record unchanged and adds all **80 nested Oregon resolution pages** selected from
nine retained 2018-2026 annual indexes. All eighty returned HTTP 200 with nonempty
extractions; raw bodies, all 697 parent-link occurrences, child links and exact
parent bindings persist. Following those pages does not exhaust their downstream links.

The expanded bounded inventory has **309 attempts, 299 retained responses and
296 distinct body hashes**. Three byte-identical pairs are retained as repeated
captures, not independent documents: California Republican URL variants, South
Carolina Democratic protocol variants and two Oklahoma Democratic PDF locations.
It accounts for all 100 expected organizations, with retained responses for 48.
The other 52 lack responses **in these selected packets**, not necessarily in the
full campaign: earlier pilot, Pennsylvania/Maryland and other recovery packets
are outside this inventory. These are not adopted-platform coverage statistics.
Five alternate OCR outputs and two public viewer exports remain separately bound
to their source bodies. All operative statuses and analytical cutoff remain unresolved.

Strict JSON parsing exposed four unrelated legacy failed-fetch records containing
nonstandard `NaN` source hashes. The new derivatives explicitly normalize only those
four fields to null and record every change; original packets remain immutable.
This differs from the legal Unicode separators repaired above. The demonstrated
NaN origin is old research-helper serialization, not an established production
collector defect. Both snapshots are strict JSON, with independently reconciled
capture identities, source/text hashes and all 100 CSV/JSON organization rows.

### HTML extraction repair and bounded source-role review

Reading actual Oregon text exposed leaked template comments and undecoded numeric
character references. Collection and homepage identity extraction now share the
existing linear block-removal helper, which also skips comments before interpreting
apparent tags inside them. HTML entities are decoded only after tag removal, preserving
literal policy comparisons such as an escaped less-than sign. Closed script contents
remain excluded even when they contain a literal comment opener. Existing handling
of unclosed block tags and the bounded-time safeguards remain covered.

A separate offline replay reproduces all eighty original extractions exactly with
the frozen old collector, then saves both complete old and repaired texts. All
eighty change; their template-comment leakage is removed. This is not a fresh
retrieval, a complete semantic cleanup or evidence of improved classifier accuracy.
Div-based page boilerplate and substantive native-unit boundaries remain unresolved.
Neither frozen captures nor canonical labels/model files were rewritten.

Six complete original texts received assistant source-role review, not topic labels.
The Joe Smith resolution's operative section is commemorative, while the Chemawa
resolution proposes a substantive governance change despite its honoring title.
Two resolutions with 2019 identifiers explicitly report approval in 2020, so
identifiers cannot substitute for adoption dates. Exact adoption and operative
evidence spans are retained in both text versions. These are source-reported
adoption claims, not independently verified events or operative-version certification.
All six remain excluded from training, model selection, independent gold and analysis.

### Current-source capability claims withheld

The legacy capability builder no longer calls heuristic-confirmed or undated
records a supported current agenda. Current stated agendas, reliable within-party
stated comparisons and operative platform coverage now remain unsupported, with
unresolved validity/coverage rather than false zeroes. Dated 2018+ records,
undated records and overlapping organization availability counts remain visible
as source inventory, without converting missing records into publication absence.

The old 30-unit reliability flag is displayed only as a legacy heuristic, not
classification or comparability validation. Regressions exercise confirmed
recent, older and undated sources while that flag is true. The analytical-depth
document now distinguishes implemented research methods from certified findings.
The later all-question capability repair described above also withholds bill/trend/
outcome/vote support, separating artifact availability into its own field. Those
analyses were not regenerated or certified by either reporting repair.

## Blocking decisions

The reviewer arrangement remains unresolved. Do not assume a human-review commitment or
independent annotation team. Evidence recovery and provisional research can continue, but
publication-quality classification certification cannot honestly be claimed without
appropriate reviewed labels and predeclared evaluation gates.
